after the theta terms are normalized such that $\mathcal{L}^{(4)} \supset \frac{\vartheta_\alpha g_\alpha^2}{32\pi^2} F_{\mu\nu}^{A_\alpha} \widetilde{F}^{A_\alpha\mu\nu}$ for each simple gauge group $G_\alpha$.

\subsubsection{Higgs-Quartic Anomalous Dimension}
\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{$\phi^4\leftarrow \phi^6$}{phi4fromphi6}.} The running of the Higgs quartic coupling induced by $Q_{H}$ is computed in Eq.~\eqref{eq:lambda_CH6}, which gives
\begin{equation}
    \frac{4!}{4}\dot{\lambda}\delta_{(ab}\delta_{cd)} = - 6! \left(-\lambda v^2 \delta_{ef}\right) \times \frac{1}{8} C_{H} \delta_{(ab}\delta_{cd}\delta_{ef)}\,.
\end{equation}
We can use 
\begin{equation}
    \delta_{ef} \delta_{(ab}\delta_{cd}\delta_{ef)} = \frac{4!}{6!} 6(4+\delta_{ee})\delta_{(ab}\delta_{cd)}\,,
\end{equation}
which results in
\begin{equation}
    \dot{\lambda} = 12\, m_H^2\, C_H\,.
\end{equation}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{$\phi^4\leftarrow \phi^2 F^2$}{phi4fromphi2F2}.} 
Operators in the class $\phi^2 F^2$ affect the running of the scalar quartic coupling according to Eq.~\eqref{eq:phi2F2_to_phi4}. In the SMEFT, the relevant operators are $Q_{HB}$, $Q_{HW}$, and $Q_{HWB}$. Applying Eq.~\eqref{eq:phi2F2_to_phi4} for the cases of $Q_{HB}$ and $Q_{HW}$ gives
\begin{align}
    \frac{4!}{4}\dot{\lambda}\delta_{(ab}\delta_{cd)} &= - 12 \sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c,d\})} \left(-\frac{1}{2}m_H^2\delta_{af}\right)\left( g_1^2\theta_{bg}^h\theta_{gf}^h\frac{1}{2}C_{HB}\delta_{cd}+g_2^2\theta_{bg}^I\theta_{gf}^J\frac{1}{2}C_{HW}\delta_{cd}\delta^{IJ}\right)\nonumber\\
    &= 18 m_H^2 \left(C_{HB} +3 C_{HW}\right)\delta_{(ab}\delta_{cd)}\,,
\end{align}
which yields
\begin{equation}
    \dot{\lambda} = 3 g_1^2 m_H^2 C_{HB} + 9 g_2^2 m_H^2 C_{HW}\,.
\end{equation}
Similarly, the case of $Q_{HWB}$ can be obtained as
\begin{align}
    \frac{4!}{4}\dot{\lambda}\delta_{(ab}\delta_{cd)} &= - 12 \sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c,d\})} g_2 g_1 \left(-\frac{1}{2}m_H^2\delta_{ae}\right) \theta_{bf}^I\theta^Y_{fe} \frac{1}{2} C_{HWB} \Sigma_{cd}^I\nonumber\\
    &= \frac{3}{4}g_1 g_2 m_H^2 C_{HWB} \sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c,d\})} \Sigma_{ab}^I \Sigma_{cd}^I\nonumber\\
    &= \frac{3}{4}g_1 g_2 m_H^2 C_{HWB}\, 4!\, \delta_{(ab}\delta_{cd)}\,,
\end{align}
which finally gives
\begin{equation}
    \dot\lambda = 3 g_1 g_2 m_{H}^2 C_{HWB}\,.
\end{equation}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{$\phi^4\leftarrow D^2 \phi^4$}{phi4fromD2phi4}.}

This anomalous dimension is reported in Eq.~\eqref{eq:phi4<-D2phi4}. 
The relevant terms on the right-hand side of this equation are
\begin{align}
    &\quad - \sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c,d\})} \Bigg[2m^2_{ef}\lambda_{gfab}\left[C_{D^2\phi^4}\right]_{egcd}\nonumber\\\nonumber 
    &\quad +\left(\frac{1}{3}m^2_{ef}\lambda_{gcab}+m^2_{gc}\lambda_{efab}\right)\left(\left[C_{D^2\phi^4}\right]_{gfed}+\left[C_{D^2\phi^4}\right]_{gefd}-\left[C_{D^2\phi^4}\right]_{fegd}\right)\\
    &\quad -6\sum_{\alpha=1}^{3} g_\alpha^2 m^2_{fg} T^\alpha_{agbe} \left[C_{D^2\phi^4}\right]_{efcd}
    -\frac{4}{3!}\lambda_{ebcd}m^2_{fg}\left(
    \left[C_{D^2\phi^4}\right]_{feag}-\left[C_{D^2\phi^4}\right]_{fgae}
    \right)
    \Bigg]\nonumber \\
    &= m_H^2\bigg[  4  \lambda \left(-26 C_{H\Box}+7C_{HD}\right)
    +\frac{3}{2}(g_1^2+g_2^2) C_{HD}
    \bigg](\delta_{ab}\delta_{cd}+\delta_{ac}\delta_{bd}+\delta_{ad}\delta_{bc})\,.\label{eq:lambdadotSMEFT1}
\end{align}
This contribution needs to be added to the one in Eq.~\eqref{eq:phi4<-Ctildeandbar} generated by $[\dot{\tildee C}_{D^2\phi^4}]_{abcd}$ and $[\dot{\overline C}_{D^2\phi^4}]_{abcd}$, which are reported in Eqs.~\eqref{eq:dotCtildeSMEFT} and \eqref{eq:dotCbarSMEFT}, respectively:
\begin{align}
    &\quad - \sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c,d\})}m^2_{ae}\left(
    \frac{1}{3}\left[\dot{\tildee C}_{D^2\phi^4}\right]_{ebcd}+\left[\dot{\overline C}_{D^2\phi^4}\right]_{ebcd}
    \right)\label{eq:lambdadotSMEFT2} \\
    &= m_H^2\bigg[  4\lambda (10 C_{H\Box}-C_{HD})+\frac{3}{2}g_1^2C_{HD}+\frac{1}{6}g_2^2(40 C_{H\Box }-27 C_{HD})
    \bigg](\delta_{ab}\delta_{cd}+\delta_{ac}\delta_{bd}+\delta_{ad}\delta_{bc})\,.\nonumber
\end{align}
By summing Eqs.~\eqref{eq:lambdadotSMEFT1} and \eqref{eq:lambdadotSMEFT2} one obtains $\dot \lambda_{abcd}$, which gives
\begin{equation}
    \dot\lambda =\frac{1}{6}m_H^2\left[4\left(5g_2^2-48\lambda\right)C_{H\Box}+9\left(
    g_1^2-g_2^2+8\lambda
    \right)C_{HD}\right] \,.
\end{equation}

\subsubsection{Higgs-Mass Anomalous Dimension}

The running of the Higgs mass $m_H^2$ only receives contributions from $D^2\phi^4$-class operators.

\paragraph{$\phi^2\leftarrow D^2\phi^4$.}
The corresponding anomalous dimension is reported in Eq.~\eqref{eq:phi2<-D2phi4}, which gives 
\begin{align}
    -\frac{1}{2}\dot{(m_H^2)}\delta_{ab} &= -\sum_{\sigma(\{a,b\})}\Bigg[2 m_{cd}^2 m_{ed}^2\left[C_{D^2\phi^4}\right]_{ceab}
    \nonumber \\
    &\quad +2m_{cd}^2 m_{ea}^2 \left(\left[C_{D^2\phi^4}\right]_{edcb}+\left[C_{D^2\phi^4}\right]_{ecdb}-\left[C_{D^2\phi^4}\right]_{dceb}\right)\nonumber \\
    &\quad-4m^2_{cb} m^2_{de}
    \left(
    \left[C_{D^2\phi^4}\right]_{dcae}-\left[C_{D^2\phi^4}\right]_{deac}
    \right)
    \Bigg] \nonumber \\
    &= m_H^4\left(2 C_{H\Box}-C_{HD}\right)\delta_{ab}\,,
\end{align}
and therefore one finds
\begin{equation}
    \dot{(m_H^2)} = m_H^4 \left(-4C_{H\Box}+2C_{HD}\right)\,.
\end{equation}


\subsection{Running in the Fermionic Sector}

Now that we have successfully derived the full RGEs in the purely bosonic sector of the SMEFT, we can turn our attention to its fermionic sector.

\subsubsection{Example: $\psi^2\phi^3$-Class Anomalous Dimension}

It is instructive to show that our results can be used to infer the running of the SMEFT operator $Q_{eH}$, which belongs to the $\psi^2\phi^3$ class, as induced by the mixing with bosonic and leptonic operators. 
This provides a particularly non-trivial example, as this class has the largest number of non-zero entries in the one-loop anomalous-dimension matrix: its running is induced by essentially all dimension-six operator classes, with the exception of $F^3$ and $\phi^6$; see Ref.~\cite{Cheung:2015aba} and the results in Section~\ref{sec:gEFT_results_psi2phi3}.

\paragraph{$\psi^2\phi^3 \leftarrow \psi^2\phi^3$.}
This anomalous dimension receives contributions of three kinds, which are proportional to the quartic Higgs coupling $\lambda$, the electron Yukawa coupling, and the gauge couplings.
We analyze each of them separately in the following.

\subparagraph{$\lambda$ dependence.}
From Eq.~\eqref{psi2phi3_psi2phi3} we have
\begin{align}
    \frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}[\dot C_{eH}]^*_{pr} \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{i*} = \frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}} \lambda [C_{eH}]^*_{pr}\sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c\})}  (\delta_{bc}\delta_{de} + \delta_{bd}\delta_{ce} + \delta_{be}\delta_{cd}) \delta_{(ad}\mathcal H_{e)}^{i *}\,.
\end{align}
Using $\delta_{(ad}\mathcal H_{d)}^{i*} = 2 \mathcal H_a^{i*}$ we obtain
\begin{equation}
    \frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}[\dot C_{eH}]^*_{pr} \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{i*} = \frac{3!}{4\sqrt{2}} \lambda [C_{eH}]^*_{pr} \left(4 \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{i*} \right)\,,
\end{equation}
which then correctly leads to \cite{Jenkins:2013zja}
\begin{equation}
    [\dot C_{eH}]_{pr} = 24\, \lambda\, [ C_{eH}]_{pr}\,.
\end{equation}

\subparagraph{Yukawa dependence.}
From Eq.~\eqref{psi2phi3_psi2phi3}, the Yukawa-dependent terms read
\begin{align}
    \frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}[\dot C_{eH}]^*_{pr} \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{i*} &= 
     2\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]^*_{pr}\mathcal H_d^{i*}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]_{st}\mathcal H_d^{j}\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}[C_{eH}]^*_{st}\delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{j*}
     \nonumber 
\\
    & + \frac{1}{2}\sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c\})}
    \left(2\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]^*_{ps}\mathcal H_d^{i*}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]_{ts}\mathcal H_c^j+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]^*_{ps}\mathcal H_c^{i*}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]_{ts}\mathcal H_d^j\right)\nonumber \\&\times \frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}[C_{eH}]^*_{tr}\delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{d)}^{j*}
    \nonumber \\
    & + \frac{1}{2}\sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c\})}
    \left(2\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]^*_{sr}\mathcal H_d^{j*}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]_{st}\mathcal H_c^j+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]^*_{sr}\mathcal H_c^{j*}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]_{st}\mathcal H_d^j\right)\nonumber \\&\times\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}[C_{eH}]^*_{pt}\delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{d)}^{i*}\nonumber \\
    &+
    \left(2\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]^*_{ps}\mathcal H_d^{i*}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]^*_{tr}\mathcal H_d^{j*} + 2 \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]^*_{pr}\mathcal H_d^{i*}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]^*_{ts}\mathcal H_d^{j*}\right)\nonumber \\&\times\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}[C_{eH}]_{ts}\delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^j
    \nonumber
\\
    &  + 3 \frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}[C_{eH}]^*_{pr}\delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{i*} [Y_e]^*_{st}[Y_e]_{st} + \frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}[C_{eH}]^*_{ps}\delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{i*}[Y_e]^*_{tr}[Y_e]_{ts}\nonumber \\&+ \frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}[C_{eH}]^*_{sr}\delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{i*}\frac{1}{2}[Y_e]_{pt}^*[Y_e]_{st} \,,
\end{align}
where the last three terms correspond to the contributions stemming from the collinear anomalous dimensions reported in Table~\ref{tab:collinear_SM}.
The last equation can be simplified as
\begin{align}
    [\dot C_{eH}]_{pr} \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{i} &= 
     [Y_e]_{pr}[Y_e]^*_{st}\mathcal H_d^{i}\mathcal H_d^{j*}[C_{eH}]_{st}\delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{j}
     \nonumber 
\\
    & + \frac{1}{4}\sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c\})}
    \left(2[Y_e]_{ps}[Y_e]^*_{ts}\mathcal H_d^{i}\mathcal H_c^{j*}+[Y_e]_{ps}[Y_e]^*_{ts}\mathcal H_c^{i}\mathcal H_d^{j*}\right) [C_{eH}]_{tr}\delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{d)}^{j}
    \nonumber \\
    & + \frac{1}{4}\sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c\})}
    \left(2[Y_e]_{sr}[Y_e]^*_{st}\mathcal H_d^{j}\mathcal H_c^{j*}+[Y_e]_{sr}[Y_e]^*_{st}\mathcal H_c^{j}\mathcal H_d^{j*}\right)[C_{eH}]_{pt}\delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{d)}^{i}\nonumber \\
    &+
    \left([Y_e]_{ps} [Y_e]_{tr}  + [Y_e]_{pr} [Y_e]_{ts} \right)\mathcal H_d^{i}\mathcal H_d^{j}[C_{eH}]^*_{ts}\delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{j*}
    \nonumber
\\
    &  + 3 [C_{eH}]_{pr}\delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{i} [Y_e]^*_{st}[Y_e]_{st} + [C_{eH}]_{ps}\delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{i}[Y_e]_{tr}[Y_e]^*_{ts}\nonumber \\&+ \frac{1}{2}[C_{eH}]_{sr}\delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{i}[Y_e]_{pt}[Y_e]_{st}^* \,.\label{eq:yukawadep_partial}
\end{align}
In the first line we can use $\mathcal H^i_d \mathcal H^{j*}_d = 2\delta_{ij}$.
In the second line we have
\begin{align}
    \sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c\})} \mathcal H_d^{i}\mathcal H_c^{j*} \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{d)}^{j} = 4\, \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H^i_{c)}\,,
    \qquad
    \sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c\})} \mathcal H_c^{i}\mathcal H_d^{j*} \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{d)}^{j} = 12\, \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H^i_{c)}\,,
\end{align}
while in the third line
\begin{align}
    \sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c\})} \mathcal H_d^{j}\mathcal H_c^{j*} \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{d)}^{i} = 4\, \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H^i_{c)}\,,
    \qquad
    \sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c\})} \mathcal H_c^{j}\mathcal H_d^{j*} \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{d)}^{i} = 8\, \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H^i_{c)}\,.
\end{align}
In the fourth line, the contraction $\mathcal H^i_d \mathcal H^j_d = 0$ correctly implies that there is no $Q_{eH} \leftarrow Q_{eH}^\dagger$ mixing.
Thus, Eq.~\eqref{eq:yukawadep_partial} leads to
\begin{align}
    [\dot C_{eH}]_{pr}
    &=
    2\, [Y_e]_{pr}\,[Y_e]^*_{st}\,[C_{eH}]_{st}
      + 
    \frac{11}{2}\,[Y_e]_{ps}\,[Y_e]^*_{ts}\, [C_{eH}]_{tr}
     + 5\,
    [Y_e]_{sr}\,[Y_e]^*_{st}\,[C_{eH}]_{pt}\nonumber \\
    &  + 3\, [Y_e]^*_{st}\,[Y_e]_{st} \,[C_{eH}]_{pr}
    \,,
\end{align}
which is consistent with \cite{Jenkins:2013wua}.

\subparagraph{Gauge-coupling dependence.}
The terms of Eq.~\eqref{psi2phi3_psi2phi3} that depend on the gauge couplings are
\begin{align}
    \frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}[\dot C_{eH}]^*_{pr} \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{i*} &=     
    - \sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c\})} 
    \left(g_1^2 \theta^Y_{bd}\theta^Y_{ce} + g_2^2 \theta^I_{bd}\theta^I_{ce} \right)
    \frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}[ C_{eH}]^*_{pr} \delta_{(ad}\mathcal H_{e)}^{i*} \nonumber 
\\
    & + 2\sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c\})}  
    \left(g_1^2 (y_{\mathsf e} + y_{\mathsf l})\delta_{ij}\theta^Y_{cd} + g_2^2 \frac{\tau^I_{ji}}{2} \theta^I_{cd}\right)
    %\sum_{\alpha=1}^{N_G} g_\alpha^2 t^{A_\alpha}_{lj} \theta^{A_\alpha}_{cd}
    \frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}[ C_{eH}]^*_{pr} \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{d)}^{j*} \nonumber \\
    & + \frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}[ C_{eH}]^*_{pr} \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{i*} \left(3\left(-3g_2^2-g_1^2\right) + \left(- 3 g_1^2\right) + \left(-\frac{9}{4}g_2^2-\frac{3}{4}g_1^2\right) \right) 
    \,,
\end{align}
where the last line corresponds to the contribution of the collinear anomalous dimensions reported in Table~\ref{tab:collinear_SM}.
The last equation can be simplified as
\begin{align}
    [\dot C_{eH}]_{pr} \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{i} &=-\frac{1}{3} [ C_{eH}]_{pr} \sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c\})} 
    \left(g_1^2 \theta^Y_{bd}\theta^Y_{ce} + g_2^2 \theta^I_{bd}\theta^I_{ce} \right)
    \left(\delta_{ad}\mathcal H^i_{e} + \delta_{ae}\mathcal H^i_{d} + \delta_{de}\mathcal H^i_{a}\right)
    \nonumber 
\\
    & - \frac{1}{3}[C_{eH}]_{pr} \sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c\})}  
    \left( g_1^2 \delta_{ij}\theta^Y_{cd} + g_2^2 \tau^I_{ij} \theta^I_{cd}\right)
    \left(\delta_{ab}\mathcal H_{d}^{j} + \delta_{ad}\mathcal H_{b}^{j} + \delta_{bd}\mathcal H_{a}^{j}\right)
    \nonumber \\
    & - [ C_{eH}]_{pr} \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{i} \left(\frac{27}{4}g_1^2 + \frac{45}{4}g_2^2 \right) \,,\label{eq:CeH<-CeH_gauge_partial}
\end{align}
where the change of sign in the second line is due to complex conjugation.
Now, using $\theta^Y_{ab}\mathcal H_b^i = - \frac{1}{2}\mathcal H^i_a$, together with 
\begin{align}
        \theta^Y_{bd}\,\theta^Y_{ce}\left(\delta_{ad}\,\mathcal{H}^i_e + \delta_{ae}\,\mathcal{H}^i_d + \delta_{de}\,\mathcal{H}^i_a\right)
    &= \frac{1}{2}\,\theta^Y_{ab}\,\mathcal{H}^i_c
    + \frac{1}{2}\,\theta^Y_{ac}\,\mathcal{H}^i_b
    - \frac{1}{4}\,\delta_{bc}\,\mathcal{H}^i_a\,,
    \\
        \theta^I_{bd}\,\theta^I_{ce}\left(\delta_{ad}\,\mathcal{H}^i_e + \delta_{ae}\,\mathcal{H}^i_d + \delta_{de}\,\mathcal{H}^i_a\right)
    &= \frac{1}{4}\,\delta_{ab}\,\mathcal{H}^i_c
    + \frac{1}{4}\,\delta_{ac}\,\mathcal{H}^i_b
    - \frac{5}{4}\,\delta_{bc}\,\mathcal{H}^i_a\,,
\end{align}
and
\begin{multline}\label{eq:gauge_H_contraction}
    \left(g_1^2\,\delta_{ij}\,\theta^Y_{cd} + g_2^2\,\tau^I_{ij}\,\theta^I_{cd}\right)
    \left(\delta_{ab}\,\mathcal{H}^j_d + \delta_{ad}\,\mathcal{H}^j_b + \delta_{bd}\,\mathcal{H}^j_a\right)
    = \\ \left(\frac{g_2^2}{2}\,\delta_{bc} - g_1^2\,\theta^Y_{bc}\right)\mathcal{H}^i_a
    + \left(\frac{g_2^2}{2}\,\delta_{ac} - g_1^2\,\theta^Y_{ac}\right)\mathcal{H}^i_b
    - \frac{5g_2^2 + g_1^2}{2}\,\delta_{ab}\,\mathcal{H}^i_c\,,
\end{multline}
Eq.~\eqref{eq:CeH<-CeH_gauge_partial} becomes
\begin{align}
    [\dot C_{eH}]_{pr} \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{i} &=-\frac{3!}{3} [ C_{eH}]_{pr} 
    \left(-\frac{1}{4}g_1^2    -\frac{3}{4} g_2^2  \right)\delta_{(bc}\mathcal H^i_{a)}
    \nonumber 
\\
    & - \frac{3!}{3}[C_{eH}]_{pr}   
    \left(-\frac{1}{2} g_1^2  - \frac{3}{2} g_2^2 \right)\delta_{(bc}\mathcal H^i_{a)}
    \nonumber \\
    & - [ C_{eH}]_{pr} \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{i} \left(\frac{27}{4}g_1^2 + \frac{45}{4}g_2^2 \right)
\end{align}
and leads to
\begin{equation}
    [\dot C_{eH}]_{pr} = -\left(\frac{21}{4}g_1^2 + \frac{27}{4}g_2^2\right) [C_{eH}]_{pr} \,,
\end{equation}
which is in agreement with \cite{Alonso:2013hga}.

\paragraph{$\psi^2\phi^3 \leftarrow \phi^2 F^2$.}
Substituting in Eq.~\eqref{psi2phi3_phi2F2} the Wilson-coefficient tensors with the expressions provided above, we obtain
\begin{align}
    \frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}[\dot C_{eH}]^*_{pr} \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{i*} &= \sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c\})}\bigg( g_1^2 (y_{\mathsf l}^2+y_{\mathsf e}^2) \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]_{pr}^* \mathcal H_a^{i*} \frac{1}{2}\delta_{bc}\left(C_{HB}+iC_{H\widetilde B}\right) \nonumber \\
    &+ g_2^2  \frac{3}{4} \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]_{pr}^* \mathcal H_a^{i*} \frac{1}{2}\delta_{bc}\left(C_{HW}+iC_{H\widetilde W}\right) \nonumber \\ &+ g_1 g_2 y_{\mathsf l} \frac{\tau^I_{ki}}{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]_{pr}^* \mathcal H_a^{i*} \frac{1}{2}\Sigma^I_{bc}\left(C_{HWB}+iC_{H\widetilde WB}\right) \bigg) \,.
\end{align}
Using in the second line $\tau^I_{ik} \tau^I_{kj} = 3\, \delta_{ij}$ and in the third line
\begin{equation}
        \tau^I_{ki}\,\mathcal{H}^{k*}_a\,\Sigma^I_{bc}
    = \mathcal{H}^{i*}_b\,\delta_{ac}
    + \mathcal{H}^{i*}_c\,\delta_{ab}
    - \mathcal{H}^{i*}_a\,\delta_{bc}
    + 2\,\mathcal{H}^{i*}_b\,\theta^Y_{ac}
    + 2\,\mathcal{H}^{i*}_c\,\theta^Y_{ab}\,,
\end{equation}
we obtain, once we add all permutations of the indices $\{a,b,c\}$ and exploit the anti-symmetry of $\theta^Y$,
\begin{align}
    \frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}[\dot C_{eH}]^*_{pr} \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{i*} &= \frac{3!}{2\sqrt{2}}g_1^2 (y_{\mathsf l}^2+y_{\mathsf e}^2) [Y_e]^*_{pr}\mathcal H_{(a}^{i*} \delta_{bc)} \left(C_{HB}+iC_{H\widetilde B}\right) \nonumber \\
    & + \frac{3!}{2\sqrt{2}}\frac{3}{4}g_2^2 [Y_e]^*_{pr}\mathcal H_{(a}^{i*} \delta_{bc)} \left(C_{HW}+iC_{H\widetilde W}\right) \nonumber \\
    & + \frac{3!}{2\sqrt{2}}\frac{1}{2}g_1 g_2 y_{\mathsf l}[Y_e]^*_{pr} \mathcal H_{(a}^{i*} \delta_{bc)} \left(C_{HWB}+iC_{H\widetilde WB}\right)\,,
\end{align}
which leads to
\begin{align}
    [\dot C_{eH}]_{pr} &= 3[Y_e]_{pr}\left(
    3g_2^2 \left(C_{HW}-iC_{H\widetilde W}\right) + 4 g_1^2 (y_{\mathsf l}^2+y_{\mathsf e}^2) \left(C_{HB}-iC_{H\widetilde B}\right)\right. \nonumber \\& \left.\,+ 2 g_1 g_2 y_{\mathsf l} \left(C_{HWB}-iC_{H\widetilde WB}\right)
    \right)\,.
\end{align}
This result is consistent with Ref.~\cite{Alonso:2013hga}, once one accounts for the different conventions for the electron Yukawa matrix and the Levi-Civita tensor, with $\epsilon^{0123} = -1$ in their case and $\epsilon^{0123} = +1$ in ours, which effectively modifies the sign of the contributions stemming from the CP-violating operators $Q_{H\widetilde W}$, $Q_{H\widetilde B}$, and $Q_{H\widetilde W B}$.

\paragraph{$\psi^2\phi^3 \leftarrow \psi^2\phi F$.}
From Eq.~\eqref{psi2phi3_psi2phiF}, this anomalous dimension is given by
\begin{align}
    \frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}[\dot C_{eH}]^*_{pr} \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{i*} &= \sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c\})} \Bigg(g_1 \Bigg(
    y_{\mathsf l}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]_{ts}\mathcal H_a^j \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]^*_{ps}\mathcal H_b^{i*}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[C_{eB}]^*_{tr}\mathcal H_c^{j*} \nonumber \\
    &- \frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]^*_{ps}\mathcal H_a^{i*} \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]_{ts}\mathcal H_d^{j}\theta^Y_{db}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[C_{eB}]^*_{tr}\mathcal H_c^{j*}
    \nonumber \\&
    -\left(g_1^2 y_{\mathsf l}\delta_{ji}\theta^Y_{da}\theta^Y_{db}+g_2^2 \frac{\tau^I_{ji}}{2}\theta^I_{da}\theta^Y_{db}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[C_{eB}]^*_{pr}\mathcal H_c^{j*}
    \nonumber \\&
    -y_{\mathsf e}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]_{ts}\mathcal H_a^j \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]^*_{tr}\mathcal H_b^{j*}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[C_{eB}]^*_{ps}\mathcal H_c^{i*}
    \nonumber \\&
    +\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]^*_{tr}\mathcal H_a^{j*} \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]_{ts}\mathcal H_d^{j}\theta^Y_{db}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[C_{eB}]^*_{ps}\mathcal H_c^{i*}\nonumber \\&
    + g_1^2 y_{\mathsf e}\theta^Y_{da}\theta^Y_{db}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[C_{eB}]^*_{pr}\mathcal H_c^{i*}
    \Bigg)
    \nonumber \\&
    +g_2 \Bigg(
    \frac{\tau^I_{li}}{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]_{ts}\mathcal H_a^j \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]^*_{ps}\mathcal H_b^{l*}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[C_{eW}]^*_{tr}\tau^I_{kj}\mathcal H_c^{k*}
    \nonumber \\&
    -\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]^*_{ps}\mathcal H_a^{i*} \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]_{ts}\mathcal H_d^{j}\theta^I_{db}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[C_{eW}]^*_{tr}\tau^I_{kj}\mathcal H_c^{k*}
    \nonumber \\&
    -\left(g_1^2 y_{\mathsf l}\delta_{ji}\theta^Y_{da}\theta^J_{db}+g_2^2 \frac{\tau^I_{ji}}{2}\theta^I_{da}\theta^J_{db}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[C_{eW}]^*_{pr}\tau^J_{kj}\mathcal H_c^{k*}
    \nonumber \\&
    +\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]^*_{tr}\mathcal H_a^{j*} \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[Y_e]_{ts}\mathcal H_d^{j}\theta^I_{db}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[C_{eW}]^*_{ps}\tau^I_{ki}\mathcal H_c^{k*}
    \nonumber \\&
    +g_1^2 y_{\mathsf e}\theta^Y_{da}\theta^I_{db}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[C_{eW}]^*_{pr}\tau^I_{ki}\mathcal H_c^{k*}
    \Bigg)
    \Bigg)
    \,,
\end{align}
which simplifies to
\begin{align}
[\dot C_{eH}]_{pr}\delta_{(ab}\mathcal{H}_{c)}^{i} &= \sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c\})}\Big(g_1\Big(
-[Y_e]^*_{ts}\mathcal{H}_a^{j*}[Y_e]_{ps}\mathcal{H}_b^{i}[C_{eB}]_{tr}\mathcal{H}_c^{j} \nonumber \\&
+ [Y_e]_{ps}\mathcal{H}_a^{i}[Y_e]^*_{ts}\mathcal{H}_d^{j*}\theta^Y_{db}[C_{eB}]_{tr}\mathcal{H}_c^{j} \nonumber \\&
- 2\left(-g_1^2 \delta_{ij}\theta^Y_{da}\theta^Y_{db} + g_2^2 \tau^{I}_{ij} \theta^I_{da}\theta^Y_{db}\right)[C_{eB}]_{pr}\mathcal{H}_c^{j} \nonumber \\&
- 2 [Y_e]^*_{ts}\mathcal{H}_a^{j*}[Y_e]_{tr}\mathcal{H}_b^{j}[C_{eB}]_{ps}\mathcal{H}_c^{i} \nonumber \\&
- [Y_e]_{tr}\mathcal{H}_a^{j}[Y_e]^*_{ts}\mathcal{H}_d^{j*}\theta^Y_{db}[C_{eB}]_{ps}\mathcal{H}_c^{i} 
+ 4 g_1^2 \theta^Y_{da}\theta^Y_{db}[C_{eB}]_{pr}\mathcal{H}_c^{i}
\Big) \nonumber \\&
+ g_2\Big(
\tau^{I}_{il}[Y_e]^*_{ts}\mathcal{H}_a^{j*}[Y_e]_{ps}\mathcal{H}_b^{l}[C_{eW}]_{tr}\tau^{I}_{jk}\mathcal{H}_c^{k} \nonumber \\&
+ [Y_e]_{ps}\mathcal{H}_a^{i}[Y_e]^*_{ts}\mathcal{H}_d^{j*}\theta^I_{db}[C_{eW}]_{tr}\tau^{I}_{jk}\mathcal{H}_c^{k} \nonumber \\&
- 2\left(-g_1^2 \delta_{ij}\theta^Y_{da}\theta^J_{db} + g_2^2\tau^{I}_{ij}\theta^I_{da}\theta^J_{db}\right)[C_{eW}]_{pr}\tau^{J}_{jk}\mathcal{H}_c^{k} \nonumber \\&
- [Y_e]_{tr}\mathcal{H}_a^{j}[Y_e]^*_{ts}\mathcal{H}_d^{j*}\theta^I_{db}[C_{eW}]_{ps}\tau^{I}_{ik}\mathcal{H}_c^{k} %\nonumber \\&
+ 4 g_1^2 \theta^Y_{da}\theta^I_{db}[C_{eW}]_{pr}\tau^{I}_{ik}\mathcal{H}_c^{k}
\Big)\Big)
\,.
\end{align}
Then, once one uses the following identities
\begin{gather}
    \tau^I_{ij}\,\theta^I_{da}\,\theta^Y_{db}\,\mathcal{H}^j_c
= \frac{1}{4}\,\delta_{ab}\,\mathcal{H}^i_c
- \frac{1}{4}\,\delta_{ac}\,\mathcal{H}^i_b
- \frac{1}{4}\,\delta_{bc}\,\mathcal{H}^i_a
- \frac{1}{2}\,\theta^Y_{ac}\,\mathcal{H}^i_b
- \frac{1}{2}\,\theta^Y_{bc}\,\mathcal{H}^i_a\,,\\
\tau^I_{il}\,\mathcal{H}^{j*}_a\,\mathcal{H}^l_b\,\tau^I_{jk}\,\mathcal{H}^k_c
= -\delta_{ac}\,\mathcal{H}^i_b
+ 2\,\delta_{ab}\,\mathcal{H}^i_c
- 2\,\theta^Y_{ac}\,\mathcal{H}^i_b
+ 4\,\theta^Y_{ab}\,\mathcal{H}^i_c\,,
\\
\mathcal{H}^i_a\,\mathcal{H}^{j*}_d\,\theta^I_{db}\,\tau^I_{jk}\,\mathcal{H}^k_c
= -\frac{3}{2}\,\delta_{bc}\,\mathcal{H}^i_a
- 3\,\theta^Y_{bc}\,\mathcal{H}^i_a\,,
\\
\delta_{ij}\,\theta^Y_{da}\,\theta^J_{db}\,\tau^J_{jk}\,\mathcal{H}^k_c
= -\frac{1}{4}\,\delta_{bc}\,\mathcal{H}^i_a
- \frac{1}{4}\,\delta_{ac}\,\mathcal{H}^i_b
+ \frac{1}{4}\,\delta_{ab}\,\mathcal{H}^i_c
- \frac{1}{2}\,\theta^Y_{bc}\,\mathcal{H}^i_a
- \frac{1}{2}\,\theta^Y_{ac}\,\mathcal{H}^i_b\,,
\\
\tau^I_{ij}\,\theta^I_{da}\,\theta^J_{db}\,\tau^J_{jk}\,\mathcal{H}^k_c
= -\frac{1}{2}\,\delta_{bc}\,\mathcal{H}^i_a
+ \frac{1}{2}\,\delta_{ac}\,\mathcal{H}^i_b
- \frac{3}{4}\,\delta_{ab}\,\mathcal{H}^i_c
- \theta^Y_{bc}\,\mathcal{H}^i_a
+ \theta^Y_{ac}\,\mathcal{H}^i_b
- \theta^Y_{ab}\,\mathcal{H}^i_c\,,
\\
\mathcal{H}^j_a\,\mathcal{H}^{j*}_d\,\theta^I_{db}\,\tau^I_{ik}\,\mathcal{H}^k_c
= -\delta_{bc}\,\mathcal{H}^i_a
+ \frac{1}{2}\,\delta_{ab}\,\mathcal{H}^i_c
- 2\,\theta^Y_{bc}\,\mathcal{H}^i_a
- \theta^Y_{ab}\,\mathcal{H}^i_c\,,
\end{gather}
and sums over all permutations of the indices $\{a,b,c\}$, one obtains
\begin{align}
[\dot C_{eH}]_{pr}\delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^i &= 3!\bigg(g_1\bigg(
-[Y_e]^*_{ts}[Y_e]_{ps}[C_{eB}]_{tr} %\nonumber \\&
-\frac{1}{2} [Y_e]_{ps}[Y_e]^*_{ts}[C_{eB}]_{tr} %\nonumber \\&
- \frac{1}{2}\left(g_1^2  - g_2^2 \right)[C_{eB}]_{pr} \nonumber \\&
- 2 [Y_e]^*_{ts}[Y_e]_{tr}[C_{eB}]_{ps} %\nonumber \\&
+\frac{1}{2} [Y_e]_{tr}[Y_e]^*_{ts}[C_{eB}]_{ps} %\nonumber \\&
- g_1^2 [C_{eB}]_{pr}\bigg) \nonumber \\&
+ g_2\bigg([Y_e]^*_{ts}[Y_e]_{ps}[C_{eW}]_{tr} %\nonumber \\&
-\frac{3}{2} [Y_e]_{ps}[Y_e]^*_{ts}[C_{eW}]_{tr} %\nonumber \\&
+ \frac{1}{2}\left(-g_1^2  +3 g_2^2\right)[C_{eW}]_{pr} \nonumber \\&
+\frac{1}{2} [Y_e]_{tr}[Y_e]^*_{ts}[C_{eW}]_{ps} %\nonumber \\&
-  g_1^2[C_{eW}]_{pr}
\bigg)\bigg)\delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^i
\,,
\end{align}
which simplifies to
\begin{align}
    [\dot C_{eH}]_{pr} &= -9\,g_1\,[Y_e]_{ps}\,[Y_e]^*_{ts}\,[C_{eB}]_{tr}-9\,g_1\,[Y_e]_{tr}\,[Y_e]^*_{ts}\,[C_{eB}]_{ps}-3\,g_1\left(3\,g_1^2-g_2^2\right)[C_{eB}]_{pr}\nonumber \\
    &-3\,g_2\,[Y_e]_{ps}\,[Y_e]^*_{ts}\,[C_{eW}]_{tr}+3\,g_2\,[Y_e]_{tr}\,[Y_e]^*_{ts}\,[C_{eW}]_{ps}-9\,g_2\left(g_1^2-g_2^2\right)[C_{eW}]_{pr}\,,
\end{align}
in agreement with \cite{Alonso:2013hga}, once one accounts for the different convention adopted for the covariant derivative, which effectively flips the sign of the gauge couplings.

\paragraph{$\psi^2\phi^3 \leftarrow \overline \psi^2\psi^2$.}
From Eq.~\eqref{psi2phi3_psibar2psi2}, this anomalous dimension is given by
\begin{align}
    \frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}[\dot C_{eH}]^*_{pr} \delta_{(ab}\mathcal H_{c)}^{i*} &=  -\frac{4}{3}\sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c\})}2 \frac{1}{\sqrt 2}[Y_e]^*_{sx}\mathcal H_c^{i*} \frac{1}{\sqrt 2}[Y_e]^*_{vt}\mathcal H_a^{j*} \frac{1}{\sqrt 2}[Y_e]_{vx}\mathcal H_b^{j}\left(-\frac{1}{4}[C_{le}]_{sprt}\right) \nonumber \\
    &+ \frac{2}{3}2\lambda (\delta_{ab}\delta_{cd}+\delta_{ac}\delta_{bd}+\delta_{ad}\delta_{bc})2\frac{1}{\sqrt 2}[Y_e]^*_{st}\mathcal H_d^{i*} \left(-\frac{1}{4}[C_{le}]_{sprt}\right) \,.
\end{align}
By using $\mathcal H_a^{j*}\mathcal H_b^j = \delta_{ab}+2\theta^Y_{ab}$ and taking the complex conjugate on both sides, we obtain
\begin{equation}
    [\dot C_{eH}]_{pr} = 8\, [Y_e]_{sx}\,[Y_e]^*_{vx}\,[Y_e]_{vt}\,[C_{le}]_{pstr} - 8\, \lambda \,[Y_e]_{st} \,[C_{le}]_{pstr}\,,
\end{equation}
in agreement with~\cite{Jenkins:2013zja,Jenkins:2013wua}.

The anomalous dimension of the operator $Q_{eH}$ receives additional contributions from the operators in the $D^2\phi^4$ and $D\overline \psi \psi \phi^2$ classes, which could also be checked with the results derived here in Section~\ref{sec:gEFTresults}.

\section{Cross-Check with the Low-Energy Effective Field Theory}
\label{sec:gEFTLEFT}

We now perform the projection of the general Lagrangian onto the LEFT, restricting our attention to the fermion masses and the dimension-five dipole operators, which correspond to operator classes that are absent in the SMEFT. 
Then, we compute the running of the gauge couplings and the topological angles, which is induced precisely by the fermion masses and dipoles.

\paragraph{Gauge group.}
The LEFT gauge group is the unbroken subgroup of the SM gauge group below the electroweak scale, i.e., a direct product of $N_G=2$ simple factors:
\begin{equation}
    G_{\text{LEFT}} = G_3 \times G_1 = SU(3)_c\times U(1)_Q\,,
\end{equation}
with gauge couplings $g_3 \equiv g$ and $g_1 \equiv e$, respectively. The adjoint indices of $SU(3)_c$ run as $A,B,\dotsc \in \{1,\dotsc,8\}$ with structure constants $f^{ABC}$.

\paragraph{Fermionic content.}
Below the electroweak scale, the SM fermion content is reduced to $n_u=2$ up-type quarks, $n_d=3$ down-type quarks, $n_e=3$ charged leptons, and $n_\nu=3$ left-handed neutrinos. The four-component Dirac fermions $u$, $d$, and $e$ are decomposed into pairs of left-handed Weyl spinors as
\begin{equation}\label{eq:LEFT_fermion_decomp}
    u_p^\alpha = \begin{pmatrix} \mathsf{u}_{Lp}^\alpha \\ \mathsf{u}_p^{\dagger\alpha} \end{pmatrix}
    \,,\qquad 
    d_p^\alpha = \begin{pmatrix} \mathsf{d}_{Lp}^\alpha \\ \mathsf{d}_p^{\dagger\alpha} \end{pmatrix}
    \,,\qquad 
    e_p = \begin{pmatrix} \mathsf{e}_{Lp} \\ \mathsf{e}_p^\dagger \end{pmatrix}\,,
\end{equation}
where $\alpha$ is the color index and $p$ the generation index, while spinor indices are kept implicit. Together with the Majorana neutrinos, this yields the following set of left-handed Weyl spinors and their representations under $G_{\text{LEFT}}$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:LEFT_fermions}
    \begin{aligned}
        \mathsf{u}_{Lp}^\alpha:& \ (\mathbf{3})_{2/3}\,,\qquad
        & \mathsf{u}_{p}^\alpha:& \ (\overline{\mathbf{3}})_{-2/3}\,,\\
        \mathsf{d}_{Lp}^\alpha:& \ (\mathbf{3})_{-1/3}\,,\qquad
        & \mathsf{d}_{p}^\alpha:& \ (\overline{\mathbf{3}})_{1/3}\,,\\
        \mathsf{e}_{Lp}:& \ (\mathbf{1})_{-1}\,,\qquad
        & \mathsf{e}_{p}:& \ (\mathbf{1})_{1}\,,\\
        \mathsf{\nu}_{Lp}:& \ (\mathbf{1})_{0}\,. & &
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Here $\mathsf{u}_p$, $\mathsf{d}_p$, and $\mathsf{e}_p$ denote the charge conjugates of the right-handed components of the corresponding Dirac fields, hence transform in the conjugate representation. The total number of independent left-handed Weyl spinor components is
\begin{equation}
    N_\psi = 2\times 3\times(n_u + n_d) + 2\times n_e + n_\nu  = 39\,,
\end{equation}
so that the collected fermion index of the general Lagrangian runs as $\psi_i$ with $i=1,\dotsc,39$.

The Hermitian generators $t^{A}$ of $SU(3)_c$ acting on the fermion fields in the collected representation are block-diagonal in the seven irreducible representations listed in Eq.~\eqref{eq:LEFT_fermions}:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:LEFT_SU3_gens}
    t^A = \text{diag}\Big(\underbrace{T^A}_{\mathsf{u}_L},\; \underbrace{-T^{A*}}_{\mathsf{u}},\; \underbrace{T^A}_{\mathsf{d}_L},\; \underbrace{-T^{A*}}_{\mathsf{d}},\; \underbrace{0}_{\mathsf{e}_L},\; \underbrace{0}_{\mathsf{e}},\; \underbrace{0}_{\mathsf{\nu}_L}\Big)\otimes\mathbb{1}_{n_\psi}\,,
\end{equation}
where $T^A=\lambda^A/2$ are the Gell-Mann generators in the fundamental representation, and $\mathbb{1}_{n_\psi}$ acts on generation space ($n_\psi$ ranging over the appropriate flavor multiplicity for each block). Similarly, the $U(1)_Q$ generator is diagonal with entries given by the electric charges of Eq.~\eqref{eq:LEFT_fermions}:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:LEFT_U1Q_gens}
    t^Q = \text{diag}\Big(\underbrace{ \tfrac{2}{3}\,\mathbb 1_3}_{\mathsf{u}_L},\; \underbrace{-\tfrac{2}{3}\,\mathbb 1_3}_{\mathsf{u}},\; \underbrace{-\tfrac{1}{3}\,\mathbb 1_3}_{\mathsf{d}_L},\; \underbrace{\tfrac{1}{3}\,\mathbb 1_3}_{\mathsf{d}},\; \underbrace{-1}_{\mathsf{e}_L},\; \underbrace{1}_{\mathsf{e}},\; \underbrace{0}_{\mathsf{\nu}_L}\Big)\otimes\mathbb{1}_{n_\psi}\,.
\end{equation}

\paragraph{Fermion masses.}
The LEFT Lagrangian contains the Dirac mass terms for the charged fermions and the Majorana mass term for the neutrinos:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:LEFT_mass}
    \mathcal{L}_{\rm mass} = -\sum_{\psi=u,d,e}\left([M_\psi]_{pr}\,\overline\psi_{Rp}\,\psi_{Lr} + \text{H.c.}\right) -\frac{1}{2}\left([M_\nu]_{pr}\,\nu_{Lp}^T\,C\,\nu_{Lr} + \text{H.c.}\right)\,,
\end{equation}
with $C=i\gamma^2\gamma^0$ and the Majorana mass matrix satisfying $M_\nu^T = M_\nu$. The matching with the general fermion mass term $\mathcal{L}^{(4)}_{\rm fer}\supset -\tfrac{1}{2!}(m_{ij}\psi_i\psi_j + \text{H.c.})$ yields
\begin{align}
    m_{(\mathsf{u}_p^\alpha)(\mathsf{u}_{Lr}^\beta)} &= m_{(\mathsf{u}_{Lr}^\beta)(\mathsf{u}_p^\alpha)} = [M_u]_{pr}\,\delta_{\alpha\beta}\,,\label{eq:Mu_matching}\\
    m_{(\mathsf{d}_p^\alpha)(\mathsf{d}_{Lr}^\beta)} &= m_{(\mathsf{d}_{Lr}^\beta)(\mathsf{d}_p^\alpha)} = [M_d]_{pr}\,\delta_{\alpha\beta}\,,\label{eq:Md_matching}\\
    m_{(\mathsf{e}_p)(\mathsf{e}_{Lr})} &= m_{(\mathsf{e}_{Lr})(\mathsf{e}_p)} = [M_e]_{pr}\,,\label{eq:Me_matching}\\
    m_{(\mathsf{\nu}_{Lp})(\mathsf{\nu}_{Lr})} &= m_{(\mathsf{\nu}_{Lr})(\mathsf{\nu}_{Lp})} = [M_\nu]_{pr}\,.\label{eq:Mnu_matching}
\end{align}
All other entries of the symmetric matrix $m_{ij}$ are zero.

\paragraph{Dipole operators.}
The LEFT contains dimension-five dipole operators. In the basis of Ref.~\cite{Jenkins:2017jig} they read as
\begin{align}
    [Q_{\nu\gamma}]_{pr} &= \nu_{Lp}^TC\Sigma^{\mu\nu}\nu_{Lr}F_{\mu\nu}\,,
    &
    [Q_{e\gamma}]_{pr} &= \overline e_{Lp}\Sigma^{\mu\nu}e_{Rr}F_{\mu\nu}\,,
    \\
    [Q_{u\gamma}]_{pr} &= \overline u_{Lp}\Sigma^{\mu\nu}u_{Rr}F_{\mu\nu}\,,
    &
    [Q_{d\gamma}]_{pr} &= \overline d_{Lp}\Sigma^{\mu\nu}d_{Rr}F_{\mu\nu}\,,
    \\
    [Q_{uG}]_{pr} &= \overline u_{Lp}\Sigma^{\mu\nu}T^A u_{Rr}G^A_{\mu\nu}\,,
    &
    [Q_{dG}]_{pr} &= \overline d_{Lp}\Sigma^{\mu\nu}T^A d_{Rr}G^A_{\mu\nu}\,.
\end{align}
Following the same steps for deriving the projection of the $\psi^2\phi F$ operator onto the dimension-six dipole operators of the SMEFT (Eqs.~\eqref{eq:CeBproj} and \eqref{eq:CeWproj}), one obtains:
\begin{align}
    \left[C_{\psi^2 F}\right]^Q_{(\nu_{Lr})(\nu_{Lp})} &= -\left[C_{\psi^2 F}\right]^Q_{(\nu_{Lp})(\nu_{Lr})} = 2\,[L_{\nu\gamma}]_{pr}\,,
    \\
    \left[C_{\psi^2 F}\right]^Q_{(\mathsf e_r)(\mathsf e_{Lp})} &= -\left[C_{\psi^2 F}\right]^Q_{(\mathsf e_{Lp})(\mathsf e_r)} = [L_{e\gamma}]_{pr}^*\,,
    \\
    \left[C_{\psi^2 F}\right]^Q_{(\mathsf u^\beta_r)(\mathsf u^\alpha_{Lp})} &= -\left[C_{\psi^2 F}\right]^Q_{(\mathsf u^\alpha_{Lp})(\mathsf u^\beta_r)} = [L_{u\gamma}]_{pr}^*\,\delta_{\alpha\beta}\,,
    \\
    \left[C_{\psi^2 F}\right]^Q_{(\mathsf d^\beta_r)(\mathsf d^\alpha_{Lp})} &= -\left[C_{\psi^2 F}\right]^Q_{(\mathsf d^\alpha_{Lp})(\mathsf d^\beta_r)} = [L_{d\gamma}]_{pr}^*\,\delta_{\alpha\beta}\,,
    \\
    \left[C_{\psi^2 F}\right]^A_{(\mathsf u^\beta_r)(\mathsf u^\alpha_{Lp})} &= -\left[C_{\psi^2 F}\right]^A_{(\mathsf u^\alpha_{Lp})(\mathsf u^\beta_r)} = [L_{uG}]_{pr}^*\,T^{A*}_{\alpha\beta}\,,
    \\
    \left[C_{\psi^2 F}\right]^A_{(\mathsf d^\beta_r)(\mathsf d^\alpha_{Lp})} &= -\left[C_{\psi^2 F}\right]^A_{(\mathsf d^\alpha_{Lp})(\mathsf d^\beta_r)} = [L_{dG}]_{pr}^*\,T^{A*}_{\alpha\beta}\,.
\end{align}

\subsubsection{Gauge-Coupling and Topological-Angle Anomalous Dimension}

From the results in Section~\ref{sunsec:gauge_topo}, one can observe that the only fermionic non-renormalizable operators that affect the running of gauge couplings and topological angles are the dimension-five dipoles, at both single and double insertion levels.

\paragraph{$F^2\leftarrow \psi^2 F$.}

From Eq.~\eqref{g_psi2F}, the running of $e$ induced by a single insertion of the dipole operators is given by
\begin{align}
    \dot e &= 
    8 e^2 \Re{[M_e]^*_{pr}(-1)(+[L_{e\gamma}]^*_{rp}) + [M_e]^*_{pr}(+1)(-[L_{e\gamma}]^*_{rp})}\nonumber \\
    &+ 8 e^2 \Re{[M_u]^*_{pr}(+\tfrac{2}{3})(+[L_{u\gamma}]^*_{rp}) + [M_u]^*_{pr}(-\tfrac{2}{3})(-[L_{u\gamma}]^*_{rp})} \times 3\nonumber \\
    &+ 8 e^2 \Re{[M_d]^*_{pr}(-\tfrac{1}{3})(+[L_{d\gamma}]^*_{rp}) + [M_d]^*_{pr}(+\tfrac{1}{3})(-[L_{d\gamma}]^*_{rp})}\times 3\nonumber \\
    &= 16e^2 \Re{-[M_e]_{pr}[L_{e\gamma}]_{rp} + 2 [M_u]_{pr}[L_{u\gamma}]_{rp} - [M_d]_{pr}[L_{d\gamma}]_{rp}}\,,
\end{align}
which agrees with Ref.~\cite{Jenkins:2017dyc}. Similarly, the corresponding running of $g$ and of the topological angles $\vartheta_{\mathrm{QED}},\vartheta_{\mathrm{QCD}}$ (obtained from Eq.~\eqref{theta_psi2F}) is given by
\begin{align}
    \dot g &= 8 g^2 \Re{[M_u]_{pr}[L_{uG}]_{rp} + [M_d]_{pr}[L_{dG}]_{rp}}\,,
    \\
    \dot \vartheta_{\mathrm{QED}} &= -\frac{256\pi^2}{e}\Im{-[M_e]_{pr}[L_{e\gamma}]_{rp} + 2 [M_u]_{pr}[L_{u\gamma}]_{rp} - [M_d]_{pr}[L_{d\gamma}]_{rp}}\,,
    \\
    \dot \vartheta_{\mathrm{QCD}} &=-\frac{128\pi^2}{g}\Im{[M_u]_{pr}[L_{uG}]_{rp} + [M_d]_{pr}[L_{dG}]_{rp}}\,.
\end{align}

\paragraph{$F^2\leftarrow \psi^2 F \times \psi^2 F$.}

From Eq.~\eqref{gauge_psi2Fxpsi2F}, the running of $e$ induced by a double insertion of the dipole operators is given by
\begin{align}
    \dot e &= 8e \Re{4[M_\nu]^*_{pr}[L_{\nu\gamma}]_{rs}[M_\nu]^*_{st}[L_{\nu\gamma}]_{tp}} + 8e \Re{[M_e]^*_{pr}[L_{e\gamma}]^*_{rs}[M_e]^*_{st}[L_{e\gamma}]^*_{tp}}\times 2 \nonumber \\
    &+ 8e \Re{[M_u]^*_{pr}[L_{u\gamma}]^*_{rs}[M_u]^*_{st}[L_{u\gamma}]^*_{tp} + [M_d]^*_{pr}[L_{d\gamma}]^*_{rs}[M_d]^*_{st}[L_{d\gamma}]^*_{tp}} \times 2 \times 3 \,,
\end{align}
which agrees again with Ref.~\cite{Jenkins:2017dyc}.
The highlighted factor of $2$ accounts for the sum of the contributions stemming from left- and right-handed fermions.
Similarly, the corresponding running of $g$ and of the topological angles $\vartheta_{\mathrm{QED}},\vartheta_{\mathrm{QCD}}$ (obtained from Eq.~\eqref{theta_psi2Fxpsi2F}) is given by
\begin{align}
    \dot g &= 8 g \Re{[M_u]_{pr}[L_{u\gamma}]_{rs}[M_u]_{st}[L_{u\gamma}]_{tp} + [M_d]_{pr}[L_{d\gamma}]_{rs}[M_d]_{st}[L_{d\gamma}]_{tp}}\,,
    \\
    \dot \vartheta_{\mathrm{QED}} &=\frac{256\pi^2}{e^2}\Im{2[M_\nu]^*_{pr}[L_{\nu\gamma}]_{rs}[M_\nu]^*_{st}[L_{\nu\gamma}]_{tp} + [M_e]^*_{pr}[L_{e\gamma}]^*_{rs}[M_e]^*_{st}[L_{e\gamma}]^*_{tp}} \nonumber \\
    &+ \frac{768\pi^2}{e^2}\Im{[M_u]^*_{pr}[L_{u\gamma}]^*_{rs}[M_u]^*_{st}[L_{u\gamma}]^*_{tp} + [M_d]^*_{pr}[L_{d\gamma}]^*_{rs}[M_d]^*_{st}[L_{d\gamma}]^*_{tp}}\,,
    \\
    \dot \vartheta_{\mathrm{QCD}} &=-\frac{128\pi^2}{g^2}\Im{[M_u]_{pr}[L_{u\gamma}]_{rs}[M_u]_{st}[L_{u\gamma}]_{tp} + [M_d]_{pr}[L_{d\gamma}]_{rs}[M_d]_{st}[L_{d\gamma}]_{tp}} \,.
\end{align}

\section{Cross-Check with the \texorpdfstring{$O(n)$}{O(n)} Effective Field Theory}
\label{sec:gEFTOn}

Finally, we consider the $O(n)$ scalar EFT. 
This theory includes $n$ real scalar gauge singlets $\phi$ and is invariant under global $O(n)$ transformations.
We consider the following dimension-six Lagrangian\footnote{The operator $(\phi \cdot \partial_\mu \phi)(\phi \cdot \partial^\mu \phi)$ is neglected since it is redundant.}
\begin{equation}
    \mathcal L_{O(n)} = \frac{1}{2}(\partial_\mu \phi)\cdot (\partial^\mu \phi) - \frac{1}{2}m^2 \phi \cdot \phi - \frac{\lambda}{4}(\phi \cdot \phi)^2 + C_{E}\phi\cdot \phi (\partial_\mu \phi)\cdot (\partial^\mu \phi) + C_1 (\phi\cdot \phi)^3\,.
\end{equation}
The renormalizable couplings of the general theory are given by
\begin{equation}
    m^2_{ab} = m^2 \,\delta_{ab}\,, \qquad 
    \lambda_{abcd} = \frac{4!}{4}\,\lambda \,\delta_{(ab}\delta_{cd)}\,,
\end{equation}
while the Wilson coefficients read
\begin{equation}
    \left[C_{D^2\phi^4}\right]_{abcd} = C_E\, \delta_{ab}\delta_{cd}\,,
    \qquad
    \left[C_{\phi^6}\right]_{abcdef} = C_1 \,\delta_{(ab}\delta_{cd}\delta_{ef)}\,.
\end{equation}
In the following, we derive the RGEs of this theory, finding full agreement with the one-loop results in~\cite{Jenkins:2023bls,Cao:2021cdt}.

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{$\phi^6 \leftarrow \phi^6$}{phi6fromphi6}.}
This anomalous dimension can be derived from Eq.~\eqref{eq:phi6<-phi6}
\begin{equation}
    \left[\dot C_{\phi^6}\right]_{abcdef} = 
    \frac{1}{2!4!}\sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c,d,e,f\})}\lambda_{efgh} \left[C_{\phi^6}\right]_{abcdgh} = 6 \lambda  (14+n) C_1 \delta_{(ab}\delta_{cd}\delta_{ef)}\,,
\end{equation}
where we exploited the identity in Eq.~\eqref{eq:identity_permutations}.
Hence, one finds
\begin{equation}
    \dot C_1 = 6\, \lambda\,  (14+n)\, C_1\,.
\end{equation}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{$\phi^4 \leftarrow \phi^6$}{phi4fromphi6}.}
From Eq.~\eqref{eq:lambda_CH6} one finds
\begin{equation}
    \dot \lambda_{abcd} = -6!\,m^2 C_1\, \delta_{ef} \delta_{(ab}\delta_{cd}\delta_{ef)} = -144 (4+n)m^2 C_1 \delta_{(ab}\delta_{cd)}\,,
\end{equation}
and therefore
\begin{equation}
    \dot \lambda = -24\, (4+n)\,m^2\, C_1 \,.
\end{equation}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{$D^2\phi^4 \leftarrow D^2\phi^4$}{D2phi4fromD2phi4}.}
From the right-hand side of Eq.~\eqref{eq:D2phi4<-D2phi4_offshell} one finds
\begin{equation}
    -\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\sigma(\{a,b\}\times \{c,d\})}\lambda_{efcd}C_E\left(\delta_{eb}\delta_{af}-\delta_{ef}\delta_{ab}\right) = 4\lambda C_E  [(1+n)\delta_{ab}\delta_{cd}-\delta_{ad}\delta_{bc}-\delta_{ac}\delta_{bd}]\,.
    \label{eq:O(n)_D2phi4}
\end{equation}
This expression allows us to extract the running for both $C_E$ and $[\tildee C_{D^2\phi^4}]_{abcd}$.
For the former, one can apply the substitution $\delta_{ad}\delta_{bc}+\delta_{ac}\delta_{bd} \to -\delta_{ab}\delta_{cd}$, which implies
\begin{equation}
    \dot C_E = 4\,\lambda\, (2+n)\, C_E\,.
\end{equation}
The anomalous dimension of the latter is obtained by subtracting $\dot C_E \delta_{ab}\delta_{cd}$ from the expression in Eq.~\eqref{eq:O(n)_D2phi4}:
\begin{align}
    \left[\dot{\tildee C}_{D^2\phi^4}\right]_{abcd} &= 4\lambda C_E  [(1+n)\delta_{ab}\delta_{cd}-\delta_{ad}\delta_{bc}-\delta_{ac}\delta_{bd}] - \dot C_E \delta_{ab}\delta_{cd}\nonumber \\
    &= -4\lambda C_E (\delta_{ab}\delta_{cd}+\delta_{ad}\delta_{bc}+\delta_{ac}\delta_{bd})\,.
    \label{eq:Ctildedot}
\end{align}
On the other hand, $[\dot{\overline C}_{D^2\phi^4}]_{abcd}$ vanishes.

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{$\phi^6\leftarrow D^2\phi^4$}{phi6fromD2phi4}.}
Using Eqs.~\eqref{eq:phi6<-D2phi4} and \eqref{eq:Ctildedot} to compute the running of $C_1$ induced by $C_E$ one finds:
\begin{align}
    \left[\dot C_{\phi^6}\right]_{abcdef} &= 
    \frac{1}{6!}\sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c,d,e,f\})}
    \bigg[
    \frac{1}{3!}\lambda_{abcg}\frac{1}{3}\left[\dot{\tildee C}_{D^2\phi^4}\right]_{gdef}
    +
    \frac{1}{2}\lambda_{ghab}\lambda_{ihcd}C_E \delta_{gi}\delta_{ef}\nonumber\\
    &\quad +\frac{1}{6}\lambda_{ghab}\lambda_{icde}C_E\left(\delta_{ih}\delta_{gf}+\delta_{ig}\delta_{hf}-\delta_{hg}\delta_{if}\right)\bigg] \nonumber \\
    &= 20 \lambda^2 C_E \delta_{(ab}\delta_{cd}\delta_{ef)}\,,
\end{align}
and therefore
\begin{equation}
    \dot C_1 = 20\, \lambda^2\, C_E\,.
\end{equation}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{$\phi^4 \leftarrow D^2\phi^4$}{phi4fromD2phi4}.}
Also for this anomalous dimension, one can exploit both Eqs.~\eqref{eq:phi4<-D2phi4} and \eqref{eq:Ctildedot}:
\begin{align}
    \dot \lambda_{abcd} &=- \sum_{\sigma(\{a,b,c,d\})} \bigg[ 
    \frac{1}{3}  m^2_{ae}\left[\dot{\tildee C}_{D^2\phi^4}\right]_{bcde} +  2 m^2_{ef}\lambda_{gfab}C_E\delta_{eg}\delta_{cd}\nonumber \\
    &\quad +\left(\frac{1}{3}m^2_{ef}\lambda_{gcab}+m^2_{gc}\lambda_{efab}\right)C_E\left(\delta_{gf}\delta_{ed}+\delta_{ge}\delta_{fd}-\delta_{fe}\delta_{gd}\right)\nonumber \\
    &\quad - \frac{4}{3!} \lambda_{ebcd} m^2_{fg}C_E \left(\delta_{fe}\delta_{ag}-\delta_{fg}\delta_{ae}\right)\bigg]\nonumber \\
    &= -96 \lambda m^2 (3+n) C_E \delta_{(ab}\delta_{cd)} \,,
\end{align}
which leads to
\begin{equation}
    \dot \lambda = -16\, \lambda\, m^2\, (3+n)\, C_E\,.
\end{equation}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{$\phi^2 \leftarrow D^2\phi^4$}{phi2fromD2phi4}.}
The running of the mass induced by $C_E$ is obtained from Eq.~\eqref{eq:phi2<-D2phi4}:
\begin{align}
    \dot {(m^2)}_{ab} &= -2 C_E \sum_{\sigma(\{a,b\})} \Big[
    m^2_{cd}m^2_{ed} \delta_{ce}\delta_{ab}+m^2_{cd}m^2_{ae}(\delta_{ed}\delta_{bc}+\delta_{ec}\delta_{bd}-\delta_{cd}\delta_{be})
    \nonumber \\
    &\quad -2  m^2_{bc} m^2_{de} \left(\delta_{cd}\delta_{ae}-\delta_{de}\delta_{ac}\right) \Big]\nonumber \\
    &= -8 n m^4 C_E \delta_{ab}\,,
\end{align}
which gives
\begin{equation}
    \dot {(m^2)} = -8\, n\, m^4\, C_E\,.
\end{equation}

\section{Conclusions}
\label{sec:gEFTconclusions}

In this chapter we have constructed the physical basis of a general effective gauge theory up to
mass dimension six using on-shell methods, adopting a general setup that includes an arbitrary
number of scalar, fermion, and vector fields transforming under arbitrary gauge groups. For this
general theory we have derived the complete set of one-loop RGEs for all renormalizable and
non-renormalizable couplings up to mass dimension six, where mixing effects between operators of
different mass dimensions were taken into account and RGE effects of order $1/\Lambda^2$ in
the EFT power counting were consistently included.

Taken together, the bosonic and fermionic results obtained here conclude the derivation of the
complete set of one-loop RGEs at the dimension-six level for relativistic EFTs containing scalars,
fermions, and vectors transforming linearly under an arbitrary gauge group; see also
Refs.~\cite{Fonseca:2025zjb,Misiak:2025xzq,Fonseca:2025cls}. Consequently, this chapter provides a general framework
for deriving the one-loop RGEs of EFTs in this broad class.

To demonstrate the utility of these results, we have reproduced the complete one-loop RGEs of the
bosonic sector of the SMEFT, together with representative results in its fermionic sector, and we
have further applied our findings to the LEFT and to the $O(n)$ scalar theory, finding agreement
with the results in the literature.

Working with a generic EFT has highlighted the advantage of performing such general computations, as
opposed to working in specific models. One clear benefit is that the RGE for a given class of
operators needs to be computed only once. For example, in the case of
$\phi^2 F^2 \leftarrow \phi^2 F^2$ the running of four SMEFT Wilson coefficients can be inferred at
once, after the general coefficient $[C_{\phi^2 F^2}]^{A_\alpha B_\beta}_{ab}$ is matched onto
$C_{HG}$, $C_{HW}$, $C_{HB}$, and $C_{HWB}$ by specifying the gauge group and the representations of
the SM fields.

The derived results open up the possibility for systematic phenomenological investigations of
light-particle extensions of the SM, in which new light degrees of freedom can imprint distinctive
signatures through renormalization group mixing into the well-constrained SMEFT
operators~\cite{Galda:2021hbr}. When such new degrees of freedom are incorporated into the EFT
framework, our results allow one to compute the corresponding RGE effects. The framework can also be
extended to include new gauge groups, such as additional $U(1)$ symmetries, which could mix with
$U(1)_Y$ and lead to non-trivial phenomenological consequences. Automating the matching of the
results derived here to specific EFTs would therefore be of immediate use, offering a systematic way
to probe light new states largely independently of their mass or lifetime. As a next step, we
envisage a software implementation performing the required group-theory calculations automatically,
in the spirit of \textsc{GroupMath}~\cite{Fonseca:2020vke}, interfaced with dedicated RGE solvers
such as \textsc{wilson}~\cite{Aebischer:2018bkb}, \textsc{RGESolver}~\cite{DiNoi:2022ejg}, or
\textsc{DsixTools}~\cite{Celis:2017hod,Fuentes-Martin:2020zaz}, enabling straightforward
phenomenological analyses and a complete streamlining of one-loop RGE calculations for gauge
theories.

Moreover, the majority of the 184 operator mixings identified, 131 in the fermionic sector and 53 in
the bosonic one, exhibit a non-trivial structure that becomes transparent within the general EFT
framework employed in this chapter, as illustrated for instance by the holomorphic combination of
$C_{\phi^2F^2}$ and $C_{\phi^2\tildee F^2}$ found in Section~\ref{sec:ex_psi2phi3}.
Finally,
the results obtained here, as well as their extensions to higher orders, may have interesting
implications for conformal field theories, where RGEs can be used to extract the scaling dimensions
of composite operators~\cite{Henriksson:2025hwi}.

Our findings have been obtained using on-shell, unitarity-based methods. As emphasized
throughout this thesis, this approach offers the advantage of reusing the same amplitudes to
determine multiple anomalous dimensions. Furthermore, the amplitudes derived here will serve as
building blocks for the calculation of two-loop anomalous dimensions, whose structural consequences
are already explored in Chapter~\ref{ch:2looptheorems}. The two-loop RGEs of a general EFT can be
derived along the same lines, as was done with diagrammatic methods for the bosonic sector in
Ref.~\cite{Guedes:2025sax}. In the case of fermionic operators, however, issues regarding evanescent
operators~\cite{Dugan:1990df,Herrlich:1994kh,Aebischer:2022aze,Aebischer:2022rxf} and
renormalization schemes~\cite{Aebischer:2023djt,Aebischer:2024xnf} need to be taken into account. We
leave this work for the future.
